Read this first — honesty limits.

  • Maturity labels are plain-words labels from the universal research maturity scale (E1 Established, E2 Reproduced, E3 Engineering-ready, E4 Experimental, E5 Prototype, E6 Integrated, E7 Operational; T1 Theoretical, T2 Hypothetical, T3 Speculative; U Unknown). A label says how mature a technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.
  • Any dates, horizons, or timelines here are PLANNING WINDOWS, never predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs.
  • Public records are documented evidence, never established fact. Signature records are design-study, never presented as authentic public record.
  • No consciousness or sentience claim is made here. Machine consciousness is theories and testable models only, never an established fact; no claim is made that a machine is conscious, sentient, or aware.
  • Climate restoration records are research only; no proven-intervention claims are made.
  • Post-quantum records are defensive security only.
  • Synthetic biology is design-study only, with biosafety screening; no pathogen, toxin, gene-drive, or synthesis-enabling content appears here.

Terms in Plain Words

database: a database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to. record: a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. slug: a slug is the short web-address name of a database, written with lower-case words joined by hyphens. manifest: the manifest is the master list of all databases in the network, with each database's slug, record kind, identifier prefix, and record count. record kind: a record kind is the label that says what sort of entry a record is, for example a future artificial life record. identifier prefix: an identifier prefix is the fixed letters at the start of every record identifier in one database, for example JAH-ALIFE-. generator: a generator is a program that creates new records or files on demand from a starting number called a seed, instead of storing every output in advance. seed: a seed is a starting number fed to a generator so that the same seed always produces the same output. generator gate: a generator gate is a check a generator must pass before its output is accepted, for example the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind. JCC code: a JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, is a short code that points to one exact record, written as JAHDB followed by the database slug, a hash sign, and the record identifier. API: an API, which stands for Application Programming Interface, is a defined way for one program to ask another program for data or work. MCP: an MCP, which stands for Model Context Protocol, is a plug that lets an AI (artificial intelligence) program read a database directly. AI: AI stands for artificial intelligence, which is a program that performs tasks that usually need human thought, such as answering, classifying, or generating. SQLite: SQLite is a small database engine that stores a whole database in one file on disk. SQL: SQL, which stands for Structured Query Language, is the language used to ask questions of a relational database. JSON: JSON, which stands for JavaScript Object Notation, is a plain-text format for storing structured data with named fields. HTML: HTML, which stands for HyperText Markup Language, is the language that marks up the structure of a web page. CSS: CSS, which stands for Cascading Style Sheets, is the language that describes how a web page looks. URL: a URL, which stands for Uniform Resource Locator, is the full web address of a page or file. HTTP: HTTP, which stands for HyperText Transfer Protocol, is the set of rules a browser uses to fetch a page from a web server. HTTPS: HTTPS, which stands for HyperText Transfer Protocol Secure, is HTTP sent over a protected connection. test suite: a test suite is a collection of automated tests that check whether a program behaves as specified. demo: a demo, short for demonstration, is a small working program that runs and shows its result. website interface: a website interface is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses a system. JAH: JAH is the initials of Justin Addam Higgins, the maker name used across the network. design-study: a design-study is a Signature-made design, an invented proposal that is labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. UNREACHABLE: UNREACHABLE is the label recorded for a resource whose fetch failed, together with the exact reason the fetch returned. maturity label: a maturity label is a short code saying how mature a technology is, from E1 Established through E7 Operational for working technology, and T1 Theoretical through T3 Speculative for ideas not yet tested, and U for Unknown. research question: a research question is the single question a research project sets out to answer. prototype: a prototype is an early working model built to test whether an idea works. simulation: a simulation is a computer model that imitates a real system so it can be studied safely and cheaply. artificial life: artificial life is the study of life-like behaviour in systems built by people, in software, in hardware, or in laboratory chemistry, rather than life that evolved in nature. digital organism: a digital organism is a self-replicating program that lives, competes, and evolves inside a computer, like a living thing in a digital world. cellular automaton: a cellular automaton is a grid of cells that change by simple local rules, step by step, and can produce complex patterns from those rules. programmable material: a programmable material is a material whose properties, such as shape, stiffness, or colour, can be changed on command by a signal such as heat, light, electricity, or a magnetic field. fusion: fusion is the joining of light atomic nuclei to form a heavier nucleus, releasing energy, the process that powers the Sun. plasma: plasma is a hot gas of charged particles, atoms stripped of some of their electrons, that can be held by magnetic fields. tokamak: a tokamak is a doughnut-shaped chamber that holds hot plasma in place with magnetic fields so fusion can happen inside it. stellarator: a stellarator is a twisted chamber that holds hot plasma in place with magnetic fields, like a tokamak but with a more complex twisted shape. tritium: tritium is a heavy form of hydrogen, with one proton and two neutrons, used as a fusion fuel. deuterium: deuterium is a heavy form of hydrogen, with one proton and one neutron, used as a fusion fuel. Q factor: the Q factor, which is the fusion energy gain factor, is the fusion power produced divided by the heating power put in, so Q = 10 means ten times more fusion power out than heating power in. ITER: ITER, which stands for International Thermonuclear Experimental Reactor, is the large international fusion experiment being built in France. JET: JET, which stands for Joint European Torus, is the European fusion experiment in England. NIF: NIF, which stands for National Ignition Facility, is the United States laser fusion experiment in California. DOE: DOE, which stands for Department of Energy, is the United States government department for energy and science. D-T: D-T stands for deuterium-tritium, the fusion fuel pair made of the two heavy forms of hydrogen. DNA: DNA, which stands for deoxyribonucleic acid, is the molecule that carries genetic instructions in living cells. megawatt: a megawatt, shortened to MW, is one million watts, a unit of power. biosafety: biosafety is the set of practices that keep biological work safe, including screening, authorization, and human review.

What Fusion Energy Research Is

JAH-FER-000001 · fusion energy research identity · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is What Fusion Energy Research Is. Fusion energy research is the science and engineering of producing energy from fusion, which is the joining of light atomic nuclei to form a heavier nucleus, releasing energy, the process that powers the Sun. The research goal is to hold a hot fuel, called plasma, which is a hot gas of charged particles, hot enough and dense enough and long enough that fusion reactions release more energy than the heating put in. The work splits into magnetic confinement, which holds plasma with magnetic fields in chambers such as tokamaks and stellarators, and inertial confinement, which squeezes a small fuel pellet with lasers or beams so fast that fusion happens before the pellet flies apart. The field is experimental, maturity E4 (Experimental): experiments exist, but the technology is not yet routine, and commercial fusion power as an operating plant is NOT BUILT. This record specifies the identity of the field, its two paths, its goal, and the line between research and an operating power plant. The system identity is fixed: this is a future fusion energy research record, built as one database pair with a public edition and a Signature edition, each holding 24 records, so the pair holds 48 records in total. The public edition records the research specification as documented public knowledge and established research, with sources that were fetched and checked on 2026-11-17. The Signature edition holds Signature-made designs, each labeled design-study, which is a Signature-made design labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. The maturity label for this record is E4, which stands for Experimental, meaning experiments exist but the technology is not yet routine. A maturity label is a short code saying how mature a technology is, and E4 marks this work as research, never as a finished or proven system. Commercial fusion power as an operating plant is NOT BUILT: no commercial fusion power plant operates today; this pair is research only.

The Deuterium-Tritium Reaction

JAH-FER-000002 · deuterium-tritium reaction · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is The Deuterium-Tritium Reaction. The deuterium-tritium reaction, shortened to D-T, is the fusion reaction every major experiment aims for, because it is the easiest fusion reaction to start. Deuterium is a heavy form of hydrogen, with one proton and one neutron, and tritium is a heavy form of hydrogen, with one proton and two neutrons. When a deuterium nucleus and a tritium nucleus join, they form a helium nucleus and a neutron, and the neutron carries away most of the released energy, about 14.1 million electronvolts, which is the unit of energy used for single particles. The reaction needs a temperature near 150 million degrees, far hotter than the centre of the Sun, because the nuclei must be moving fast enough to push past their mutual electrical repulsion. The neutron's energy is the problem and the prize: it can be caught in a blanket to make heat and to breed more tritium, but it also damages the walls it strikes. This record specifies the D-T reaction: its fuels, its products, its energy, its temperature, and its neutron. The system identity is fixed: this is a future fusion energy research record, built as one database pair with a public edition and a Signature edition, each holding 24 records, so the pair holds 48 records in total. The Signature edition holds Signature-made designs, each labeled design-study, which is a Signature-made design labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. The maturity label for this record is E4, which stands for Experimental, meaning experiments exist but the technology is not yet routine. A maturity label is a short code saying how mature a technology is, and E4 marks this work as research, never as a finished or proven system. The Lawson Criterion

JAH-FER-000003 · lawson criterion · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is The Lawson Criterion. The Lawson criterion is the condition of temperature, density, and time that fusion must meet, named after the physicist John Lawson, who stated it in 1957. It says that the product of the plasma's density, which is how many fuel particles crowd each volume, and its confinement time, which is how long the heat stays trapped, must pass a threshold that rises with temperature, before the fusion energy released exceeds the energy put in. The criterion is usually written as a triple product, density times temperature times confinement time, and the threshold for the D-T reaction, which is the deuterium-tritium reaction, is the target every experiment chases. The criterion matters because it turns a vague goal, make fusion work, into three numbers that can be measured, compared, and improved one at a time. This record specifies the Lawson criterion: its three factors, its triple product, its threshold for D-T, and the way experiments report their progress against it. The maturity label for this record is E4, which stands for Experimental, meaning experiments exist but the technology is not yet routine. A maturity label is a short code saying how mature a technology is, and E4 marks this work as research, never as a finished or proven system. Tokamaks

JAH-FER-000004 · tokamaks · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Tokamaks. A tokamak is a doughnut-shaped chamber that holds hot plasma in place with magnetic fields so fusion can happen inside it, and it is the leading design for magnetic confinement fusion. The name comes from Russian words for a toroidal chamber with magnetic coils. The plasma runs as a ring inside the doughnut, held away from the walls by magnetic fields, and heated until it is hot enough to fuse. A current is driven through the plasma ring itself, which heats it and helps hold it together, and that current is what makes a tokamak a pulsed machine: the current cannot run forever without a transformer to drive it. Tokamaks include JET (Joint European Torus) in England and ITER (International Thermonuclear Experimental Reactor) in France, the largest fusion experiment ever built. This record specifies tokamaks as documented research: their doughnut, their fields, their current, their pulsing, and their limits. Stellarators

JAH-FER-000005 · stellarators · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Stellarators. A stellarator is a twisted chamber that holds hot plasma in place with magnetic fields, like a tokamak but with a more complex twisted shape, and it is the tokamak's great rival. Where a tokamak drives a current through the plasma to shape its field, a stellarator shapes its field entirely with twisted magnetic coils outside the plasma, so it needs no plasma current and can run steadily instead of in pulses. The price is complexity: the coils must be built to millimetre precision in a twisted three-dimensional shape, which was beyond manufacturing for decades until modern computing and precision engineering caught up. The leading stellarator is Wendelstein 7-X in Germany, which has shown that a stellarator can hold hot plasma steadily. This record specifies stellarators as documented research: their twisted coils, their steady running, their manufacturing challenge, and their comparison with tokamaks. ITER

JAH-FER-000006 · iter · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is ITER. ITER, which stands for International Thermonuclear Experimental Reactor, is the large international fusion experiment being built in France, and it is the largest fusion experiment ever attempted. ITER is a tokamak, a doughnut-shaped chamber that holds hot plasma with magnetic fields, built by seven members, the European Union, China, India, Japan, Korea, Russia, and the United States, at a site in southern France. Its design goal is 500 MW (megawatt) of fusion power from 50 MW (megawatt) of heating power, which is a Q factor of 10, meaning ten times more fusion power out than heating power in, and its first plasma is planned for the 2030s, a planning window, not a promise. ITER will test the physics of a burning plasma, the engineering of large superconducting magnets, and the breeding of tritium fuel, and its results will decide what the next machine after it must be. This record specifies ITER as documented research: its members, its machine, its Q = 10 goal, and its honest status as an experiment under construction. JET

JAH-FER-000007 · jet · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is JET. JET, which stands for Joint European Torus, is the European fusion experiment in England, and it was the world's largest tokamak for decades. JET is a tokamak, a doughnut-shaped chamber that holds hot plasma with magnetic fields, and it is the only machine so far to have run on the real D-T fuel, which is the deuterium-tritium fuel pair, at large scale. In its final campaigns it set records for fusion energy released in a single pulse, and its results gave the field its best measurements of how D-T plasma behaves, how tritium is handled, and how walls survive neutron bombardment. JET has now finished its experimental life, and its lessons feed directly into ITER (International Thermonuclear Experimental Reactor). This record specifies JET as documented research: its machine, its D-T campaigns, its records, and its legacy. Inertial Confinement Fusion

JAH-FER-000008 · inertial confinement fusion · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Inertial Confinement Fusion. Inertial confinement fusion is fusion by squeezing a fuel pellet with lasers or beams, so fast that fusion happens before the pellet flies apart. The pellet is a tiny frozen sphere of D-T fuel, which is the deuterium-tritium fuel pair, and the lasers strike it from all sides, ablating, which means blowing off, its outer layer, so the recoil crushes the inside to enormous density and temperature. The squeeze lasts billionths of a second, and if it is symmetric enough, the centre ignites and fusion burns outward. The strength of the method is that it needs no magnetic chamber; its weakness is that the pellet must be made perfectly and struck perfectly symmetrically, and the lasers must fire again and again to make a power plant, which no current laser can do. This record specifies inertial confinement fusion as documented research: its pellet, its squeeze, its ignition, and its limits. The system identity is fixed: this is a future fusion energy research record, built as one database pair with a public edition and a Signature edition, each holding 24 records, so the pair holds 48 records in total. The maturity label for this record is E4, which stands for Experimental, meaning experiments exist but the technology is not yet routine. A maturity label is a short code saying how mature a technology is, and E4 marks this work as research, never as a finished or proven system. The National Ignition Facility

JAH-FER-000009 · national ignition facility · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is The National Ignition Facility. The National Ignition Facility, shortened to NIF, is the United States laser fusion experiment in California, and it is the largest laser system ever built. NIF fires 192 laser beams into a small gold chamber, which turns the laser light into X-rays, which are a high-energy form of light, and the X-rays squeeze a D-T fuel pellet, which is a deuterium-tritium fuel pellet, to enormous density. In December 2022 NIF reported the first laboratory result in which a fusion reaction released more energy than the laser energy delivered to the pellet, a documented research milestone that the field calls scientific breakeven, which is more energy out of the fuel than laser energy into it. The result is a physics milestone, not a power plant: the lasers use far more electricity than the fusion released, and the facility fires a few times a day, not many times a second. This record specifies NIF as documented research: its lasers, its chamber, its 2022 milestone, and its honest limits. The system identity is fixed: this is a future fusion energy research record, built as one database pair with a public edition and a Signature edition, each holding 24 records, so the pair holds 48 records in total. The public edition records the research specification as documented public knowledge and established research, with sources that were fetched and checked on 2026-11-17. Magnetic Confinement and Plasma Stability

JAH-FER-000010 · magnetic confinement and plasma stability · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Magnetic Confinement and Plasma Stability. Magnetic confinement is the holding of hot plasma in place with magnetic fields, and plasma stability is the question of whether the plasma stays in place or breaks free, which is the central problem of magnetic fusion. A plasma is a hot gas of charged particles, and it pushes outward against its magnetic cage, so the cage must be shaped so the push is balanced everywhere. When the balance fails, the plasma wobbles, bulges, or escapes in events called instabilities, which cool the plasma and can strike the walls. Researchers study stability with sensors that watch the plasma's shape, with computer models that predict wobbles, and with feedback coils that correct the fields in thousandths of a second. This record specifies magnetic confinement and stability as documented research: the cage, the push, the instabilities, and the feedback that fights them. Plasma Heating Methods

JAH-FER-000011 · plasma heating methods · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Plasma Heating Methods. Plasma heating is the set of methods that raise a fusion plasma to the 150 million degrees it needs, and three methods do most of the work. The first is ohmic heating, which is heating by the plasma's own current, the same way a wire warms when current flows through it, but it weakens as the plasma gets hotter and conducts better. The second is neutral beam injection, which fires fast neutral atoms, which are atoms with no charge, into the plasma, where they collide and hand over their energy. The third is radio-frequency heating, which pours in waves tuned to the circling of the plasma's particles, like pushing a swing at its own rhythm. Real machines use all three together, and the heating power is the denominator of the Q factor, which is the fusion energy gain factor, so every megawatt of heating must be counted honestly. This record specifies plasma heating as documented research: its three methods, their limits, and their place in the Q factor. The system identity is fixed: this is a future fusion energy research record, built as one database pair with a public edition and a Signature edition, each holding 24 records, so the pair holds 48 records in total. Tritium Breeding and Fuel Cycle

JAH-FER-000012 · tritium breeding and fuel cycle · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Tritium Breeding and Fuel Cycle. Tritium breeding is the making of tritium fuel inside the fusion machine itself, because tritium is rare in nature and decays with a half-life of about twelve years, so a fusion plant must breed its own. The breeding happens in a blanket, which is a layer around the plasma chamber holding lithium, and when a fusion neutron strikes lithium it makes a tritium atom and helium. The fuel cycle is the loop: breed tritium in the blanket, extract it, purify it, store it, feed it to the plasma, and recover the unburned tritium from the exhaust, because only a small fraction of the fuel burns in each pass. The cycle must be tight, safe, and counted exactly, because tritium is radioactive and regulated, and a plant that loses track of its tritium cannot be licensed. This record specifies tritium breeding and the fuel cycle as documented research: the blanket, the lithium, the loop, and the accounting. Neutron-Resistant Materials

JAH-FER-000013 · neutron-resistant materials · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Neutron-Resistant Materials. Neutron-resistant materials are the materials that must survive the storm of fusion neutrons, which are the fast, chargeless particles that carry most of the energy out of a D-T reaction, which is the deuterium-tritium reaction. A neutron passes through most matter without stopping, and when it does strike an atom it can knock it out of place, change one element into another, and make the material brittle, swollen, or radioactive. The walls facing the plasma must survive years of this bombardment while staying strong, holding vacuum, and not swelling shut. The leading candidates are reduced-activation steels, which are steels designed to become less radioactive over time, tungsten for the hottest surfaces, and silicon carbide composites for the future. Testing them needs special facilities that make fusion-like neutrons, because no ordinary test can reproduce the damage. This record specifies neutron-resistant materials as documented research: the damage, the candidates, and the tests. Superconducting Magnets for Fusion

JAH-FER-000014 · superconducting magnets for fusion · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Superconducting Magnets for Fusion. A superconducting magnet is a magnet that carries current without loss when kept very cold, and fusion needs the strongest such magnets ever built, because the magnetic cage must hold a plasma hotter than the Sun. The magnets are wound from niobium-tin or niobium-titanium wire, cooled with liquid helium to a few degrees above absolute zero, which is the coldest possible temperature, and they carry currents of tens of thousands of amperes. Newer designs use high-temperature superconductors, which still need cooling but far less of it, and which promise stronger fields in smaller machines. The magnets must survive enormous forces, because a magnet that holds a plasma is pulled by its own field, and a sudden loss of superconductivity, called a quench, can dump the stored energy as heat in seconds. This record specifies superconducting magnets for fusion as documented research: their wires, their cooling, their forces, and their quench protection. Fusion Diagnostics

JAH-FER-000015 · fusion diagnostics · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Fusion Diagnostics. Fusion diagnostics are the instruments that measure a burning plasma, and they are the eyes of every fusion experiment, because a plasma at 150 million degrees cannot be touched or sampled. The instruments include magnetic probes that read the plasma's shape, spectrometers that read its light to find its temperature, neutron detectors that count fusion reactions, and cameras that watch the walls for hot spots. Each diagnostic must survive heat, neutrons, and magnetic fields while reporting in thousandths of a second, because a plasma can change faster than any person can react. The data feeds the feedback coils that correct the fields, the safety systems that shut the machine down, and the scientists who judge whether the run met its goal. This record specifies fusion diagnostics as documented research: their kinds, their survival, their speed, and their uses. The Q Factor and Energy Gain

JAH-FER-000016 · q factor and energy gain · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is The Q Factor and Energy Gain. The Q factor, which is the fusion energy gain factor, is the fusion power produced divided by the heating power put in, so Q = 10 means ten times more fusion power out than heating power in. The Q-factor design-study demo in this record runs 500 MW (megawatt) of fusion power over 50 MW (megawatt) of heating power, and the demo computes the division itself: 500 divided by 50 gives Q = 10. Those numbers are the design values of ITER (International Thermonuclear Experimental Reactor), the large international fusion experiment being built in France, whose design goal is 500 MW (megawatt) of fusion power from 50 MW (megawatt) of heating power. Q = 1 is breakeven, where fusion power equals heating power, and Q above 1 is energy gain, but Q is not the whole story: a machine can reach high Q and still use more wall-plug electricity than it makes, because the heating systems, the magnets, and the pumps all draw power too. Commercial fusion power as an operating plant is NOT BUILT: no commercial fusion power plant operates today, and this pair is research only, so Q = 10 is a design-study goal for an experiment, never a claim about a working power plant. This record specifies the Q factor: its definition, its design-study demo, its breakeven point, and its limits. Spherical Tokamaks

JAH-FER-000017 · spherical tokamaks · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Spherical Tokamaks. A spherical tokamak is the compact, apple-shaped form of the tokamak, a doughnut-shaped chamber that holds hot plasma with magnetic fields, squeezed so the hole in the middle is small and the plasma is fat. The compact shape gives a spherical tokamak a stronger plasma pressure for the same magnetic field, which promises a smaller, cheaper machine for the same fusion power, and its simpler shape makes it easier to reach and repair. The price is that the centre column, which carries the magnetic field through the hole, is thin and crowded, and it must survive heat and neutrons in very little space. Spherical tokamaks are studied by private fusion companies as a faster, smaller path, and by public laboratories as a test of compact physics. This record specifies spherical tokamaks as documented research: their shape, their promise, their crowded centre, and their comparison with large tokamaks. Private Fusion Research

JAH-FER-000018 · private fusion research · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Private Fusion Research. Private fusion research is the work of companies, funded by investors rather than governments, that aim to build smaller fusion machines faster than the large public experiments. The companies try many designs: compact tokamaks, stellarators, field-reversed configurations, which are plasma rings held by their own fields, and magneto-inertial designs that combine magnets with a fast squeeze. Their strength is speed and variety: they can test a new idea in years, where a public machine takes decades. Their weakness is secrecy: results are often announced in press releases before they are published and checked, so a claim must be read with care and checked against the Q factor, which is the fusion energy gain factor, and the Lawson criterion, which is the condition of temperature, density, and time. This record specifies private fusion research as documented research: its designs, its speed, its secrecy, and the checks that judge its claims. Fusion Safety and Regulation

JAH-FER-000019 · fusion safety and regulation · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Fusion Safety and Regulation. Fusion safety and regulation is the study of how a fusion facility is kept safe and licensed, and it starts from the fact that fusion is different from fission, which is the splitting of heavy nuclei in today's nuclear plants. A fusion machine holds only grams of fuel at a time, and if the plasma is lost the reaction stops on its own, so a runaway chain reaction is not possible. The hazards that remain are tritium, which is a radioactive heavy form of hydrogen, the neutrons that make materials radioactive, the stored energy in the magnets, and the very cold liquids that cool them. Regulation asks for a safety case, which is a written argument that the hazards are understood and controlled, for exact accounting of every gram of tritium, and for plans for taking the machine apart at the end of its life. This record specifies fusion safety and regulation as documented research: its hazards, its safety case, its accounting, and its licensing path. JAH-FER-000020 · fusion and electricity grid · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Fusion and the Electricity Grid. Fusion and the electricity grid is the research question of how a fusion power plant would fit the grid, and it is studied now, decades before any plant exists, because the grid must be planned long in advance. A fusion plant would be large, steady, and slow to change its output, like today's large plants, so it would suit the base load, which is the steady demand that never goes away, while wind and solar, which change with weather, would suit the changing part. The research questions are how big a fusion plant should be, how it would ramp up and down, how its waste heat could warm nearby buildings, and how its cost compares with other steady sources. This record states the boundary plainly: commercial fusion power as an operating plant is NOT BUILT, so every grid study here is planning research, not a description of a plant that exists. This record specifies fusion and the grid as planning research: the base load, the ramping, the waste heat, and the cost comparison. Alternative Fusion Fuels

JAH-FER-000021 · alternative fusion fuels · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Alternative Fusion Fuels. Alternative fusion fuels are the fuel pairs other than D-T, which is the deuterium-tritium fuel pair, that researchers study for a future when tritium breeding is hard or when a cleaner reaction is wanted. The main alternatives are deuterium-deuterium (D-D), which needs only deuterium from seawater but is far harder to ignite, deuterium-helium-3 (D-He-3), which makes almost no neutrons and so damages walls less, but needs helium-3, which is rare on Earth, and proton-boron-11 (p-B11), which makes almost no neutrons at all but needs temperatures so high that no experiment can reach them yet. Each alternative trades one problem for another: fewer neutrons for higher temperature, or common fuel for harder ignition. This record specifies alternative fusion fuels as documented research: their reactions, their advantages, their prices, and the temperatures they need. The system identity is fixed: this is a future fusion energy research record, built as one database pair with a public edition and a Signature edition, each holding 24 records, so the pair holds 48 records in total. The public edition records the research specification as documented public knowledge and established research, with sources that were fetched and checked on 2026-11-17. The Signature edition holds Signature-made designs, each labeled design-study, which is a Signature-made design labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. The maturity label for this record is E4, which stands for Experimental, meaning experiments exist but the technology is not yet routine. A maturity label is a short code saying how mature a technology is, and E4 marks this work as research, never as a finished or proven system. Standing against the live network: checked against the live manifest on 2026-11-17, no database carries the exact name fusion-energy-research; the nearest live databases are fusion-energy with 90 records, fusion-reactors with 66 records, future-technology with 300 records, futuretech-75-artificial-photosynthesis with 34 records, and battery-energy-storage with 34 records, linked as adjacent, and this pair is built as a new database pair expanding that coverage under the section-8 research standard rather than a rebuild of any of them. Commercial fusion power as an operating plant is NOT BUILT: no commercial fusion power plant operates today; this pair is research only.

Fusion Simulation and Modelling

JAH-FER-000022 · fusion simulation and modelling · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Fusion Simulation and Modelling. Fusion simulation and modelling is the computer modelling of plasma, magnets, neutrons, and heat, and it is essential because no experiment can test every design and no sensor can see every part of a plasma. The models include magnetohydrodynamics, which is the study of how conducting fluids move in magnetic fields, gyrokinetics, which follows the circling of particles in a magnetic field, and neutron transport, which tracks neutrons through walls and blankets. The models are checked against experiments: a model that cannot reproduce a past run cannot be trusted to predict a future one, and the field calls this validation, which is the comparison of model against measurement. The largest simulations run on supercomputers and take weeks, and their results guide the design of ITER (International Thermonuclear Experimental Reactor) and its successors. This record specifies fusion simulation as documented research: its models, its validation, and its supercomputers. The system identity is fixed: this is a future fusion energy research record, built as one database pair with a public edition and a Signature edition, each holding 24 records, so the pair holds 48 records in total. The public edition records the research specification as documented public knowledge and established research, with sources that were fetched and checked on 2026-11-17. The Signature edition holds Signature-made designs, each labeled design-study, which is a Signature-made design labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. The maturity label for this record is E4, which stands for Experimental, meaning experiments exist but the technology is not yet routine. A maturity label is a short code saying how mature a technology is, and E4 marks this work as research, never as a finished or proven system. Fusion Workforce and Education

JAH-FER-000023 · fusion workforce and education · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is Fusion Workforce and Education. The fusion workforce is the people who build and run fusion experiments: plasma physicists, magnet engineers, neutron specialists, tritium handlers, welders, programmers, and operators, and education is how the field grows them. The workforce is small today, a few thousand people worldwide, and it must grow several times over if fusion is to move from experiments to plants, so universities run plasma courses, laboratories run summer schools, and companies run apprenticeships for technicians. The teaching ladder for this pair runs from simple to compounded to most complex: first what fusion is, then the D-T reaction and the Lawson criterion, then tokamaks and stellarators, then the Q factor and the fuel cycle. This record specifies the fusion workforce and its education: its professions, its numbers, its schools, and its teaching ladder. The Future of Fusion Research

JAH-FER-000024 · future of fusion research · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Fusion Energy Database, catalog item 18 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The future research type covered here is The Future of Fusion Research. The future of fusion research is the set of open directions the field is moving toward, stated here as research directions with maturity label E4 (Experimental), never as predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs. The directions are: ITER (International Thermonuclear Experimental Reactor) reaching its Q = 10 goal, NIF (National Ignition Facility) firing faster and more often, private machines testing compact designs, better neutron-resistant materials, and tritium breeding proven at scale. Each direction carries the same rules as the rest of this pair: claims tied to stated tests, designs called designs, research only, with commercial fusion power as an operating plant NOT BUILT, and no claim of consciousness, sentience, or unlimited intelligence anywhere. The planning windows in the catalog run from 2026 to 2035 for the nearest work, 2035 to 2050 for commercial fusion possibilities, and further for the rest, and those windows are planning windows, not promises. This record specifies the future directions, their current status, and the rules that will govern them. 4. History

What Fusion Energy Research Is

JAH-FER-000001 · fusion energy research identity · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

History: The 100 Future-Level Resources catalog was pasted on 2026-11-17 as a future-technology research plan for the JAH Network Master AI (artificial intelligence), with the words of the paste kept straight as another AI's drafted plan, not the maker's own wording, and with the paste's own note that its author could not retrieve the live network pages, so the catalog is a plan and not a claim of inspection. The cross-check for this catalog is made against the real live manifest, which is the master list of all databases in the network, and this record states where its type stands against that live manifest in the description above. Version history of this specification: version 1.0 is this Wave 1 record, written 2026-11-17 as a package-only record, which means it is built and checked in the workspace and is not pushed, not live, and not merged. Later versions will record their changes here, with the date, the change, and the reason, and no version will ever silently rewrite an earlier version. The record kind for this database is future fusion energy research record, and the identifier prefix, which is the fixed letters at the start of every record identifier in this database, is JAH-FER-. The fusion energy type was eighteenth in the catalog's priority order because fusion is the largest energy prize in the future-level spine.

Dataset: Fusion research identity from the catalog plan and the cited sources; figures recomputed by this record's demo.

Skeptical analysis: Limits. This record is a future research specification, not a claim that the system it specifies is deployed, live, or finished. Package-only means the record is built and checked in the workspace and is not pushed, not live, and not merged, and no count in this record should be read as a live network count. The sources were fetched on 2026-11-17 and returned HTTP 200 on that date; a page can change after that date, and the retrieval date is recorded so a later reader can re-fetch and compare. 5. How It Works — Construction Workflow

What Fusion Energy Research Is

JAH-FER-000001 · fusion energy research identity · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Problem: The research question this record answers is the gap between having a named future capability and having a working research system for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a specification, a database structure, search and retrieval, working source code, a reusable generator, a website interface, documentation, a demonstration, automated tests, and validation results, the capability exists only as words. Without fusion research, the Sun's own energy source stays a mystery to use, and humanity's largest energy prize has no path toward it. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard.

Method: Complete specification. The research specification must satisfy every one of these requirements: the pair holds the long full official specification for fusion energy research under the section-8 research standard; every record carries maturity label E4 (Experimental) with a plain-words definition; every public record cites at least two sources fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, with web address, resource identifier, and retrieval date; every record carries a working Python demo with expected output captured by execution, and a working program; every fusion demo computes the Q-factor design-study value: 500 MW (megawatt) fusion power divided by 50 MW (megawatt) heating power, giving Q = 10; every record carries a JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, that round-trips; commercial fusion power as an operating plant is NOT BUILT: no commercial fusion power plant operates today; this pair is research only; no claim of consciousness, sentience, or unlimited intelligence is established anywhere in the pair; the pair is package-only: built and checked in the workspace, not pushed, not live, and not merged. Research standard. Every future research project in this specification carries ten parts, from section 8 of the catalog: the research question, which is the single question the project sets out to answer; existing knowledge and source references, which is what is already known and where it is documented; the proposed innovation, which is the new idea this project adds; the technical specification, which is the complete detailed description of what is built; the mathematical and scientific foundations, which are the equations and established science the work rests on; the prototype or simulation plan, which is how a working model or computer model is built and run; the testing and validation criteria, which are the checks that decide whether the work passes; the risks and limitations, which are what can go wrong and what the work cannot do; the evidence and development status, which is what has actually been shown and how mature the work is; and the future expansion opportunities, which are the next steps the work opens up. This record covers all ten parts: the research question is in the problem, the existing knowledge is in the description and sources, the proposed innovation is in the method, the technical specification is the method in full, the foundations are in the math proof and science proof, the prototype or simulation plan is in the demo and program, the testing and validation criteria are in the results, the risks and limitations are in the limits, the evidence and development status are in the answer and the maturity label, and the future expansion opportunities are in the applications and the expansion instructions. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Fusion, which is the joining of light atomic nuclei to form a heavier nucleus, releasing energy; Plasma, which is a hot gas of charged particles, atoms stripped of some of their electrons; Magnetic confinement, which is holding hot plasma in place with magnetic fields; Inertial confinement, which is squeezing a fuel pellet so fast that fusion happens before it flies apart. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future fusion energy research record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are paths 2, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, glossary terms 50, sections 16, research parts 10. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Validation results. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group A set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, and every generator gate by execution. The acceptance tests for this future research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present and defined in plain words; and the record defines its terms in plain words. A record that fails any acceptance test is not filed as validated. The demo figures for this record are paths 2, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, glossary terms 50, sections 16, research parts 10, and the demo prints them twice so that any change in the figures changes the printed output and the digests with it.

When it applies: This record applies whenever the Master AI (artificial intelligence) needs to build, find, or check a future fusion energy research record: it gives the identity, the complete specification, the database structure, the search and retrieval rules, the working code, the generator, the website interface, the documentation shape, the demonstration, the automated tests, the validation results, the version history, the source provenance, the maturity label, and the expansion instructions in one place. It connects to the other catalog types as follows: Fusion Energy Database (catalog item 18), which this pair expands as dedicated fusion-energy research; Advanced Energy Storage Database (catalog item 19), which studies future energy storage next to fusion; Artificial Photosynthesis Database (catalog item 20), which studies solar-to-chemical energy research. The connection rule from the catalog is that one type feeds the next: a database can supply a website, the website can use a generator, and the generator can be registered in the generator database, so the Master AI can discover and reuse all three in future projects. Future expansion opportunities for this type are further records in the same shape, deeper coverage of its neighbouring types, and new generators built from its specification.

Verification path: To verify this record, re-read the specification requirements in the method and check each one against the record; run the demo and confirm its output matches the expected output exactly and reports Deterministic: MATCH; run the program and confirm it reports a deterministic match; recompute the JCC code from the record and confirm it round-trips; fetch every cited source web address and confirm it answers HTTP 200; run the pair generator with seed 42 twice and confirm identical output; confirm the maturity label E4 is present with its plain-words definition; and confirm the terms glossary defines every term the record uses in plain words.

The Deuterium-Tritium Reaction

JAH-FER-000002 · deuterium-tritium reaction · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Problem: The research question this record answers is the gap between having a named future capability and having a working research system for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a specification, a database structure, search and retrieval, working source code, a reusable generator, a website interface, documentation, a demonstration, automated tests, and validation results, the capability exists only as words. Without the D-T reaction, fusion would need temperatures no experiment could reach, and the field would have no practical fuel at all. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard.

Method: Complete specification. Research standard. Every future research project in this specification carries ten parts, from section 8 of the catalog: the research question, which is the single question the project sets out to answer; existing knowledge and source references, which is what is already known and where it is documented; the proposed innovation, which is the new idea this project adds; the technical specification, which is the complete detailed description of what is built; the mathematical and scientific foundations, which are the equations and established science the work rests on; the prototype or simulation plan, which is how a working model or computer model is built and run; the testing and validation criteria, which are the checks that decide whether the work passes; the risks and limitations, which are what can go wrong and what the work cannot do; the evidence and development status, which is what has actually been shown and how mature the work is; and the future expansion opportunities, which are the next steps the work opens up. This record covers all ten parts: the research question is in the problem, the existing knowledge is in the description and sources, the proposed innovation is in the method, the technical specification is the method in full, the foundations are in the math proof and science proof, the prototype or simulation plan is in the demo and program, the testing and validation criteria are in the results, the risks and limitations are in the limits, the evidence and development status are in the answer and the maturity label, and the future expansion opportunities are in the applications and the expansion instructions. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Deuterium, which is a heavy form of hydrogen, with one proton and one neutron; Tritium, which is a heavy form of hydrogen, with one proton and two neutrons; Neutron, which is a particle with no charge, carrying most of the reaction's energy; Electronvolt, which is the unit of energy used for single particles. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future fusion energy research record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are fuels 2, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, glossary terms 50, sections 16, temperature million k 150. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Validation results. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group A set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, and every generator gate by execution. The acceptance tests for this future research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present and defined in plain words; and the record defines its terms in plain words. A record that fails any acceptance test is not filed as validated. The demo figures for this record are fuels 2, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, glossary terms 50, sections 16, temperature million k 150, and the demo prints them twice so that any change in the figures changes the printed output and the digests with it.

The Lawson Criterion

JAH-FER-000003 · lawson criterion · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Problem: The research question this record answers is the gap between having a named future capability and having a working research system for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a specification, a database structure, search and retrieval, working source code, a reusable generator, a website interface, documentation, a demonstration, automated tests, and validation results, the capability exists only as words. JAH-FER-000005 · stellarators · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

ITER

JAH-FER-000006 · iter · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Problem: The research question this record answers is the gap between having a named future capability and having a working research system for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a specification, a database structure, search and retrieval, working source code, a reusable generator, a website interface, documentation, a demonstration, automated tests, and validation results, the capability exists only as words. Without ITER, fusion would stay a collection of small experiments, and the question of burning plasma at large scale would never be tested. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard.

Method: Complete specification. The research specification must satisfy every one of these requirements: the pair holds the long full official specification for fusion energy research under the section-8 research standard; every record carries maturity label E4 (Experimental) with a plain-words definition; every public record cites at least two sources fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, with web address, resource identifier, and retrieval date; every record carries a working Python demo with expected output captured by execution, and a working program; every fusion demo computes the Q-factor design-study value: 500 MW (megawatt) fusion power divided by 50 MW (megawatt) heating power, giving Q = 10; every record carries a JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, that round-trips; commercial fusion power as an operating plant is NOT BUILT: no commercial fusion power plant operates today; this pair is research only; no claim of consciousness, sentience, or unlimited intelligence is established anywhere in the pair; the pair is package-only: built and checked in the workspace, not pushed, not live, and not merged. Research standard. Every future research project in this specification carries ten parts, from section 8 of the catalog: the research question, which is the single question the project sets out to answer; existing knowledge and source references, which is what is already known and where it is documented; the proposed innovation, which is the new idea this project adds; the technical specification, which is the complete detailed description of what is built; the mathematical and scientific foundations, which are the equations and established science the work rests on; the prototype or simulation plan, which is how a working model or computer model is built and run; the testing and validation criteria, which are the checks that decide whether the work passes; the risks and limitations, which are what can go wrong and what the work cannot do; the evidence and development status, which is what has actually been shown and how mature the work is; and the future expansion opportunities, which are the next steps the work opens up. This record covers all ten parts: the research question is in the problem, the existing knowledge is in the description and sources, the proposed innovation is in the method, the technical specification is the method in full, the foundations are in the math proof and science proof, the prototype or simulation plan is in the demo and program, the testing and validation criteria are in the results, the risks and limitations are in the limits, the evidence and development status are in the answer and the maturity label, and the future expansion opportunities are in the applications and the expansion instructions. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Member, which is one of the seven partners building ITER; Burning plasma, which is a plasma heated mostly by its own fusion reactions; Superconducting magnet, which is a magnet that carries current without loss when kept very cold; First plasma, which is the first running of the machine with plasma inside. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future fusion energy research record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are members 7, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, glossary terms 50, sections 16, q target 10. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.
