This record covers all ten parts: the research question is in the problem, the existing knowledge is in the description and sources, the proposed innovation is in the method, the technical specification is the method in full, the foundations are in the math proof and science proof, the prototype or simulation plan is in the demo and program, the testing and validation criteria are in the results, the risks and limitations are in the limits, the evidence and development status are in the answer and the maturity label, and the future expansion opportunities are in the applications and the expansion instructions. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Laser beam, which is a focused beam of light; Gold chamber, which is the small gold container that turns laser light into X-rays; X-ray, which is a high-energy form of light; Scientific breakeven, which is more fusion energy out of the fuel than laser energy into it. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future fusion energy research record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are laser beams 192, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, glossary terms 50, sections 16, year milestone 2022. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Validation results. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group A set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, and every generator gate by execution. The acceptance tests for this future research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present and defined in plain words; and the record defines its terms in plain words. A record that fails any acceptance test is not filed as validated. The demo figures for this record are laser beams 192, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, glossary terms 50, sections 16, year milestone 2022, and the demo prints them twice so that any change in the figures changes the printed output and the digests with it.

When it applies: This record applies whenever the Master AI (artificial intelligence) needs to build, find, or check a future fusion energy research record: it gives the identity, the complete specification, the database structure, the search and retrieval rules, the working code, the generator, the website interface, the documentation shape, the demonstration, the automated tests, the validation results, the version history, the source provenance, the maturity label, and the expansion instructions in one place. It connects to the other catalog types as follows: Fusion Energy Database (catalog item 18), which this pair expands as dedicated fusion-energy research; Advanced Energy Storage Database (catalog item 19), which studies future energy storage next to fusion; Artificial Photosynthesis Database (catalog item 20), which studies solar-to-chemical energy research. The connection rule from the catalog is that one type feeds the next: a database can supply a website, the website can use a generator, and the generator can be registered in the generator database, so the Master AI can discover and reuse all three in future projects. Future expansion opportunities for this type are further records in the same shape, deeper coverage of its neighbouring types, and new generators built from its specification.

Magnetic Confinement and Plasma Stability

JAH-FER-000010 · magnetic confinement and plasma stability · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Problem: The research question this record answers is the gap between having a named future capability and having a working research system for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a specification, a database structure, search and retrieval, working source code, a reusable generator, a website interface, documentation, a demonstration, automated tests, and validation results, the capability exists only as words. Without stability, a magnetic cage holds its plasma for a moment and loses it the next, and no fusion reaction can last long enough to matter. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard.

Working source code. Reusable generator. JAH-FER-000016 · q factor and energy gain · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Q factor, which is the fusion energy gain factor, fusion power divided by heating power; Breakeven, which is Q = 1, where fusion power equals heating power; Megawatt, which is one million watts, a unit of power, shortened to MW; Wall-plug electricity, which is the electricity drawn from the grid by the whole facility. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future fusion energy research record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are fusion power mw 500, heating power mw 50, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, glossary terms 50, sections 16, q target 10. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Validation results. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group A set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, and every generator gate by execution. Research standard. Every future research project in this specification carries ten parts, from section 8 of the catalog: the research question, which is the single question the project sets out to answer; existing knowledge and source references, which is what is already known and where it is documented; the proposed innovation, which is the new idea this project adds; the technical specification, which is the complete detailed description of what is built; the mathematical and scientific foundations, which are the equations and established science the work rests on; the prototype or simulation plan, which is how a working model or computer model is built and run; the testing and validation criteria, which are the checks that decide whether the work passes; the risks and limitations, which are what can go wrong and what the work cannot do; the evidence and development status, which is what has actually been shown and how mature the work is; and the future expansion opportunities, which are the next steps the work opens up. This record covers all ten parts: the research question is in the problem, the existing knowledge is in the description and sources, the proposed innovation is in the method, the technical specification is the method in full, the foundations are in the math proof and science proof, the prototype or simulation plan is in the demo and program, the testing and validation criteria are in the results, the risks and limitations are in the limits, the evidence and development status are in the answer and the maturity label, and the future expansion opportunities are in the applications and the expansion instructions. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Grid, which is the network that carries electricity to users; Base load, which is the steady demand that never goes away; Ramping, which is the changing of output up or down; Waste heat, which is heat thrown away by a power plant. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future fusion energy research record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are grid parts 3, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, glossary terms 50, sections 16, plant sizes 2. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Validation results. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group A set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, and every generator gate by execution. The acceptance tests for this future research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present and defined in plain words; and the record defines its terms in plain words. A record that fails any acceptance test is not filed as validated. The demo figures for this record are grid parts 3, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, glossary terms 50, sections 16, plant sizes 2, and the demo prints them twice so that any change in the figures changes the printed output and the digests with it.

A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Validation results. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group A set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, and every generator gate by execution. The acceptance tests for this future research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present and defined in plain words; and the record defines its terms in plain words. A record that fails any acceptance test is not filed as validated. The demo figures for this record are professions 7, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, glossary terms 50, sections 16, ladder steps 4, and the demo prints them twice so that any change in the figures changes the printed output and the digests with it.

When it applies: This record applies whenever the Master AI (artificial intelligence) needs to build, find, or check a future fusion energy research record: it gives the identity, the complete specification, the database structure, the search and retrieval rules, the working code, the generator, the website interface, the documentation shape, the demonstration, the automated tests, the validation results, the version history, the source provenance, the maturity label, and the expansion instructions in one place. Future expansion opportunities for this type are further records in the same shape, deeper coverage of its neighbouring types, and new generators built from its specification.

The Future of Fusion Research

JAH-FER-000024 · future of fusion research · Maturity: E4 — Experimental — experiments exist, not yet routine.

Problem: The research question this record answers is the gap between having a named future capability and having a working research system for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a specification, a database structure, search and retrieval, working source code, a reusable generator, a website interface, documentation, a demonstration, automated tests, and validation results, the capability exists only as words. Without stated future directions, a research field drifts, and funders, students, and builders cannot tell which work matters next. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard.

Method: Complete specification. The research specification must satisfy every one of these requirements: the pair holds the long full official specification for fusion energy research under the section-8 research standard; every record carries maturity label E4 (Experimental) with a plain-words definition; every public record cites at least two sources fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, with web address, resource identifier, and retrieval date; every record carries a working Python demo with expected output captured by execution, and a working program; every fusion demo computes the Q-factor design-study value: 500 MW (megawatt) fusion power divided by 50 MW (megawatt) heating power, giving Q = 10; every record carries a JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, that round-trips; commercial fusion power as an operating plant is NOT BUILT: no commercial fusion power plant operates today; this pair is research only; no claim of consciousness, sentience, or unlimited intelligence is established anywhere in the pair; the pair is package-only: built and checked in the workspace, not pushed, not live, and not merged. Research standard. Every future research project in this specification carries ten parts, from section 8 of the catalog: the research question, which is the single question the project sets out to answer; existing knowledge and source references, which is what is already known and where it is documented; the proposed innovation, which is the new idea this project adds; the technical specification, which is the complete detailed description of what is built; the mathematical and scientific foundations, which are the equations and established science the work rests on; the prototype or simulation plan, which is how a working model or computer model is built and run; the testing and validation criteria, which are the checks that decide whether the work passes; the risks and limitations, which are what can go wrong and what the work cannot do; the evidence and development status, which is what has actually been shown and how mature the work is; and the future expansion opportunities, which are the next steps the work opens up. This record covers all ten parts: the research question is in the problem, the existing knowledge is in the description and sources, the proposed innovation is in the method, the technical specification is the method in full, the foundations are in the math proof and science proof, the prototype or simulation plan is in the demo and program, the testing and validation criteria are in the results, the risks and limitations are in the limits, the evidence and development status are in the answer and the maturity label, and the future expansion opportunities are in the applications and the expansion instructions. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Direction, which is one open line of research the field is moving toward; Planning window, which is a date range used for planning, not a prediction or a promise; Breeding at scale, which is making tritium in a full-size blanket; Compact design, which is a smaller fusion machine. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future fusion energy research record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are directions 5, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, glossary terms 50, sections 16, planning windows 4. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. 7. AI

The AI of this database is JAH Fusion Energy Research Librarian. It explains this database in plain, conversational language, answers questions about any of the 24 records, and can create an AI assistant exactly like itself. It answers from this database's own data only, and says plainly when a question goes beyond it.

  • ai-thing.json — the per-database AI spec (the database's own AI, JAH Fusion Energy Research Librarian).
  • ai-discussion.json — the discussion AI spec, built to discuss Fusion Energy Database with a visitor, record by record.

Both are free downloads, free forever, no sign-in.
